I've uploaded a python code repository in the directory juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7. Consider the following issue description:

<issue_description>
Handle optional scope on token and introspection endpoints for password and client_credentials grant_type
Parameter scope is defined as optional on authorization https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-4.1.1 and introspection https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7662 requests. 

An enhancement is proposed to handle `scope` parameter on authorization requests with grant_type=password or grant_type= client_credentials (related to https://github.com/juanifioren/django-oidc-provider/pull/193):
1) if `scope` is not provided client's scope(s) are assigned to access token - 

- grant_type=client_credentials: current behavior of django-oidc-provider
- grant_type=password: currently django-oidc-provider assigns empty scope

2) if valid (supported by given client) scopes are requested requested scopes are assigned to access token
3) if requested scope(s) are not supported `raise TokenError('invalid_scope')`

An enhancement is proposed to display optional `scope` in introspection response controlled by new settings variable `OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE`. 
</issue_description>

Can you help me implement the necessary changes to the repository so that the requirements specified in the <issue_description> are met?

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me carefully read and understand the issue description:

1. The issue is about handling optional `scope` parameter on token and introspection endpoints for password and client_credentials grant types.

2. According to RFC specifications:
   - Scope is optional on authorization requests (RFC 6749 Section 4.1.1)
   - Scope is optional on introspection requests (RFC 7662)

3. The enhancements needed are:
   - Handle `scope` parameter on authorization requests with grant_type=password or grant_type=client_credentials
   - If scope is not provided: assign client's scope(s) to access token
     - For client_credentials: current behavior (already implemented)
     - For password: currently assigns empty scope (needs to be fixed to assign client scopes)
   - If valid scopes are requested: assign requested scopes to access token
   - If requested scopes are not supported: raise TokenError('invalid_scope')
   - Display optional scope in introspection response controlled by new settings variable OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE

Key technical details:
- Methods/grant_types involved: password, client_credentials
- Error to raise: TokenError('invalid_scope')
- New setting: OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE
- Current behavior: client_credentials works correctly, password assigns empty scope

     8	## About OpenID
     9	
    10	OpenID Connect is a simple identity layer on top of the OAuth 2.0 protocol, which allows computing clients to verify the identity of an end-user based on the authentication performed by an authorization server, as well as to obtain basic profile information about the end-user in an interoperable and REST-like manner. Like [Google](https://developers.google.com/identity/protocols/OpenIDConnect) for example.
    11

    13	
    14	`django-oidc-provider` can help you providing out of the box all the endpoints, data and logic needed to add OpenID Connect (and OAuth2) capabilities to your Django projects.
    15	
    16	Support for Python 3 and 2. Also latest versions of django.

    16	    create_token,
    17	    encode_id_token,
    18	)
    19	from oidc_provider.models import (
    20	    Client,
    21	    Code,
    22	    Token,
    23	)
    24	from oidc_provider import settings
    25	
    26	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    27	
    28	
    29	class TokenEndpoint(object):
    30	
    31	    def __init__(self, request):
    32	        self.request = request
    33	        self.params = {}
    34	        self.user = None
    35	        self._extract_params()
    36	
    37	    def _extract_params(self):
    38	        client_id, client_secret = extract_client_auth(self.request)
    39	
    40	        self.params['client_id'] = client_id
    41	        self.params['client_secret'] = client_secret
    42	        self.params['redirect_uri'] = self.request.POST.get('redirect_uri', '')
    43	        self.params['grant_type'] = self.request.POST.get('grant_type', '')
    44	        self.params['code'] = self.request.POST.get('code', '')
    45	        self.params['state'] = self.request.POST.get('state', '')
    46	        self.params['scope'] = self.request.POST.get('scope', '')
    47	        self.params['refresh_token'] = self.request.POST.get('refresh_token', '')
    48	        # PKCE parameter.
    49	        self.params['code_verifier'] = self.request.POST.get('code_verifier')
    50	
    51	        self.params['username'] = self.request.POST.get('username', '')
    52	        self.params['password'] = self.request.POST.get('password', '')
    53	
    54	    def validate_params(self):
    55	        try:
    56	            self.client = Client.objects.get(client_id=self.params['client_id'])
    57	        except Client.DoesNotExist:
    58	            logger.debug('[Token] Client does not exist: %s', self.params['client_id'])
    59	            raise TokenError('invalid_client')
    60	
    61	        if self.client.client_type == 'confidential':
    62	            if not (self.client.client_secret == self.params['client_secret']):
    63	                logger.debug('[Token] Invalid client secret: client %s do not have secret %s',
    64	                             self.client.client_id, self.client.client_secret)
    65	                raise TokenError('invalid_client')
    66	
    67	        if self.params['grant_type'] == 'authorization_code':
    68	            if not (self.params['redirect_uri'] in self.client.redirect_uris):
    69	                logger.debug('[Token] Invalid redirect uri: %s', self.params['redirect_uri'])
    70	                raise TokenError('invalid_client')
    71	
    72	            try:
    73	                self.code = Code.objects.get(code=self.params['code'])
    74	            except Code.DoesNotExist:
    75	                logger.debug('[Token] Code does not exist: %s', self.params['code'])
    76	                raise TokenError('invalid_grant')
    77	
    78	            if not (self.code.client == self.client) \
    79	               or self.code.has_expired():
    80	                logger.debug('[Token] Invalid code: invalid client or code has expired')
    81	                raise TokenError('invalid_grant')
    82	
    83	            # Validate PKCE parameters.
    84	            if self.params['code_verifier']:
    85	                if self.code.code_challenge_method == 'S256':
    86	                    new_code_challenge = urlsafe_b64encode(
    87	                            hashlib.sha256(self.params['code_verifier'].encode('ascii')).digest()
    88	                        ).decode('utf-8').replace('=', '')
    89	                else:
    90	                    new_code_challenge = self.params['code_verifier']
    91	
    92	                # TODO: We should explain the error.
    93	                if not (new_code_challenge == self.code.code_challenge):
    94	                    raise TokenError('invalid_grant')
    95	
    96	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'password':
    97	            if not settings.get('OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE'):
    98	                raise TokenError('unsupported_grant_type')
    99	
   100	            auth_args = (self.request,)
   101	            try:
   102	                inspect.getcallargs(authenticate, *auth_args)
   103	            except TypeError:
   104	                auth_args = ()
   105	
   106	            user = authenticate(
   107	                *auth_args,
   108	                username=self.params['username'],
   109	                password=self.params['password']
   110	            )
   111	
   112	            if not user:
   113	                raise UserAuthError()
   114	
   115	            self.user = user
   116	
   117	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'refresh_token':
   118	            if not self.params['refresh_token']:
   119	                logger.debug('[Token] Missing refresh token')
   120	                raise TokenError('invalid_grant')
   121	
   122	            try:
   123	                self.token = Token.objects.get(refresh_token=self.params['refresh_token'],
   124	                                               client=self.client)
   125	
   126	            except Token.DoesNotExist:
   127	                logger.debug(
   128	                    '[Token] Refresh token does not exist: %s', self.params['refresh_token'])
   129	                raise TokenError('invalid_grant')
   130	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'client_credentials':
   131	            if not self.client._scope:
   132	                logger.debug('[Token] Client using client credentials with empty scope')
   133	                raise TokenError('invalid_scope')
   134	        else:
   135	            logger.debug('[Token] Invalid grant type: %s', self.params['grant_type'])
   136	            raise TokenError('unsupported_grant_type')
   137	
   138	    def create_response_dic(self):
   139	        if self.params['grant_type'] == 'authorization_code':
   140	            return self.create_code_response_dic()
   141	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'refresh_token':
   142	            return self.create_refresh_response_dic()
   143	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'password':
   144	            return self.create_access_token_response_dic()
   145	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'client_credentials':
   146	            return self.create_client_credentials_response_dic()
   147	
   148	    def create_code_response_dic(self):
   149	        # See https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-4.1
   150	
   151	        token = create_token(
   152	            user=self.code.user,
   153	            client=self.code.client,
   154	            scope=self.code.scope)
   155	
   156	        if self.code.is_authentication:
   157	            id_token_dic = create_id_token(
   158	                user=self.code.user,
   159	                aud=self.client.client_id,
   160	                token=token,
   161	                nonce=self.code.nonce,
   162	                at_hash=token.at_hash,
   163	                request=self.request,
   164	                scope=token.scope,
   165	            )
   166	        else:
   167	            id_token_dic = {}
   168	        token.id_token = id_token_dic
   169	
   170	        # Store the token.
   171	        token.save()
   172	
   173	        # We don't need to store the code anymore.
   174	        self.code.delete()
   175	
   176	        dic = {
   177	            'access_token': token.access_token,
   178	            'refresh_token': token.refresh_token,
   179	            'token_type': 'bearer',
   180	            'expires_in': settings.get('OIDC_TOKEN_EXPIRE'),
   181	            'id_token': encode_id_token(id_token_dic, token.client),
   182	        }
   183	
   184	        return dic
   185	
   186	    def create_refresh_response_dic(self):
   187	        # See https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-6
   188	
   189	        scope_param = self.params['scope']
   190	        scope = (scope_param.split(' ') if scope_param else self.token.scope)
   191	        unauthorized_scopes = set(scope) - set(self.token.scope)
   192	        if unauthorized_scopes:
   193	            raise TokenError('invalid_scope')
   194	
   195	        token = create_token(
   196	            user=self.token.user,
   197	            client=self.token.client,
   198	            scope=scope)
   199	
   200	        # If the Token has an id_token it's an Authentication request.
   201	        if self.token.id_token:
   202	            id_token_dic = create_id_token(
   203	                user=self.token.user,
   204	                aud=self.client.client_id,
   205	                token=token,
   206	                nonce=None,
   207	                at_hash=token.at_hash,
   208	                request=self.request,
   209	                scope=token.scope,
   210	            )
   211	        else:
   212	            id_token_dic = {}
   213	        token.id_token = id_token_dic
   214	
   215	        # Store the token.
   216	        token.save()
   217	
   218	        # Forget the old token.
   219	        self.token.delete()
   220	
   221	        dic = {
   222	            'access_token': token.access_token,
   223	            'refresh_token': token.refresh_token,
   224	            'token_type': 'bearer',
   225	            'expires_in': settings.get('OIDC_TOKEN_EXPIRE'),
   226	            'id_token': encode_id_token(id_token_dic, self.token.client),
   227	        }
   228	
   229	        return dic
   230	
   231	    def create_access_token_response_dic(self):
   232	        # See https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-4.3
   233	
   234	        token = create_token(
   235	            self.user,
   236	            self.client,
   237	            self.params['scope'].split(' '))
   238	
   239	        id_token_dic = create_id_token(
   240	            token=token,
   241	            user=self.user,
   242	            aud=self.client.client_id,
   243	            nonce='self.code.nonce',
   244	            at_hash=token.at_hash,
   245	            request=self.request,
   246	            scope=token.scope,
   247	        )
   248	
   249	        token.id_token = id_token_dic
   250	        token.save()
   251	
   252	        return {
   253	            'access_token': token.access_token,
   254	            'refresh_token': token.refresh_token,
   255	            'expires_in': settings.get('OIDC_TOKEN_EXPIRE'),
   256	            'token_type': 'bearer',
   257	            'id_token': encode_id_token(id_token_dic, token.client),
   258	        }
   259	
   260	    def create_client_credentials_response_dic(self):
   261	        # See https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-4.4.3
   262	
   263	        token = create_token(
   264	            user=None,
   265	            client=self.client,
   266	            scope=self.client.scope)
   267	
   268	        token.save()
   269	
   270	        return {
   271	            'access_token': token.access_token,
   272	            'expires_in': settings.get('OIDC_TOKEN_EXPIRE'),
   273	            'token_type': 'bearer',
   274	            'scope': self.client._scope,
   275	        }
   276	
   277	    @classmethod
   278	    def response(cls, dic, status=200):
   279	        """
   280	        Create and return a response object.
   281	        """
   282	        response = JsonResponse(dic, status=status)
   283	        response['Cache-Control'] = 'no-store'
   284	        response['Pragma'] = 'no-cache'
   285	
   286	        return response
   287

     1	import logging
     2	
     3	from django.http import JsonResponse
     4	
     5	from oidc_provider.lib.errors import TokenIntrospectionError
     6	from oidc_provider.lib.utils.common import run_processing_hook
     7	from oidc_provider.lib.utils.oauth2 import extract_client_auth
     8	from oidc_provider.models import Token, Client
     9	from oidc_provider import settings
    10	
    11	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    12	
    13	INTROSPECTION_SCOPE = 'token_introspection'
    14	
    15	
    16	class TokenIntrospectionEndpoint(object):
    17	
    18	    def __init__(self, request):
    19	        self.request = request
    20	        self.params = {}
    21	        self.token = None
    22	        self.id_token = None
    23	        self.client = None
    24	        self._extract_params()
    25	
    26	    def _extract_params(self):
    27	        # Introspection only supports POST requests
    28	        self.params['token'] = self.request.POST.get('token')
    29	        client_id, client_secret = extract_client_auth(self.request)
    30	        self.params['client_id'] = client_id
    31	        self.params['client_secret'] = client_secret
    32	
    33	    def validate_params(self):
    34	        if not (self.params['client_id'] and self.params['client_secret']):
    35	            logger.debug('[Introspection] No client credentials provided')
    36	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    37	        if not self.params['token']:
    38	            logger.debug('[Introspection] No token provided')
    39	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    40	        try:
    41	            self.token = Token.objects.get(access_token=self.params['token'])
    42	        except Token.DoesNotExist:
    43	            logger.debug('[Introspection] Token does not exist: %s', self.params['token'])
    44	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    45	        if self.token.has_expired():
    46	            logger.debug('[Introspection] Token is not valid: %s', self.params['token'])
    47	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    48	
    49	        try:
    50	            self.client = Client.objects.get(
    51	                client_id=self.params['client_id'],
    52	                client_secret=self.params['client_secret'])
    53	        except Client.DoesNotExist:
    54	            logger.debug('[Introspection] No valid client for id: %s',
    55	                         self.params['client_id'])
    56	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    57	        if INTROSPECTION_SCOPE not in self.client.scope:
    58	            logger.debug('[Introspection] Client %s does not have introspection scope',
    59	                         self.params['client_id'])
    60	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    61	
    62	        self.id_token = self.token.id_token
    63	
    64	        if settings.get('OIDC_INTROSPECTION_VALIDATE_AUDIENCE_SCOPE'):
    65	            if not self.token.id_token:
    66	                logger.debug('[Introspection] Token not an authentication token: %s',
    67	                             self.params['token'])
    68	                raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    69	
    70	            audience = self.token.id_token.get('aud')
    71	            if not audience:
    72	                logger.debug('[Introspection] No audience found for token: %s',
    73	                             self.params['token'])
    74	                raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    75	
    76	            if audience not in self.client.scope:
    77	                logger.debug('[Introspection] Client %s does not audience scope %s',
    78	                             self.params['client_id'], audience)
    79	                raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    80	
    81	    def create_response_dic(self):
    82	        response_dic = {}
    83	        if self.id_token:
    84	            for k in ('aud', 'sub', 'exp', 'iat', 'iss'):
    85	                response_dic[k] = self.id_token[k]
    86	        response_dic['active'] = True
    87	        response_dic['client_id'] = self.token.client.client_id
    88	
    89	        response_dic = run_processing_hook(response_dic,
    90	                                           'OIDC_INTROSPECTION_PROCESSING_HOOK',
    91	                                           client=self.client,
    92	                                           id_token=self.id_token)
    93	
    94	        return response_dic
    95	
    96	    @classmethod
    97	    def response(cls, dic, status=200):
    98	        """
    99	        Create and return a response object.
   100	        """
   101	        response = JsonResponse(dic, status=status)
   102	        response['Cache-Control'] = 'no-store'
   103	        response['Pragma'] = 'no-cache'
   104	
   105	        return response
   106

   153	
   154	        From the specification:
   155	            Since this access token request utilizes the resource owner's
   156	            password, the authorization server MUST protect the endpoint
   157	            against brute force attacks (e.g., using rate-limitation or
   158	            generating alerts).
   159	
   160	        How you do this, is up to you.

   162	        return False

   174	
   175	def import_from_str(value):
   176	    """
   177	    Attempt to import a class from a string representation.
   178	    """
   179	    try:
   180	        parts = value.split('.')
   181	        module_path, class_name = '.'.join(parts[:-1]), parts[-1]
   182	        module = importlib.import_module(module_path)
   183	        return getattr(module, class_name)
   184	    except ImportError as e:
   185	        msg = 'Could not import %s for settings. %s: %s.' % (value, e.__class__.__name__, e)
   186	        raise ImportError(msg)
   187	
   188	
   189	def get(name, import_str=False):
   190	    """
   191	    Helper function to use inside the package.
   192	    """
   193	    value = None
   194	    default_value = getattr(default_settings, name)
   195	
   196	    try:
   197	        value = getattr(settings, name)
   198	    except AttributeError:
   199	        if name in default_settings.required_attrs:
   200	            raise Exception('You must set ' + name + ' in your settings.')
   201

   207	            value = default_value

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me check what the PR #193 mentioned in the issue was about:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Based on my exploration so far, I can see the key files that need to be modified:

1. **Main Issue Analysis**:
   - The problem is about handling optional `scope` parameter on token and introspection endpoints for password and client_credentials grant types
   - Currently:
     - For client_credentials: Works correctly (assigns client scopes)
     - For password: Assigns empty scope when no scope provided (should assign client scopes)

2. **Files to Modify**:
   - `/oidc_provider/lib/endpoints/token.py` - Main token endpoint logic
   - `/oidc_provider/lib/endpoints/introspection.py` - Introspection endpoint logic
   - `/oidc_provider/settings.py` - Need to add new setting OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE

3. **Key Functions to Modify**:
   - In `token.py`: `create_access_token_response_dic()` method (password grant) and `validate_params()` method (scope validation)
   - In `introspection.py`: `create_response_dic()` method (to optionally include scope in response)

4. **Current Behavior Analysis**:
   - Line 237 in token.py: `self.params['scope'].split(' ')` - always splits scope even when empty
   - Line 266 in token.py: `self.client.scope` - correctly uses client scope for client_credentials
   - For password grants, when no scope is provided, it becomes an empty list which is wrong

5. **Requirements**:
   - If scope not provided: assign client's scope(s) to access token
   - If valid scopes requested: assign requested scopes to access token  
   - If requested scopes not supported: raise TokenError('invalid_scope')
   - Add optional scope display in introspection response controlled by new settings variable

Let me create a detailed plan for the implementation.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Create Reproduction Script

Let me first create a simple test to understand the current behavior and then verify our fix:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7/test_scope_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python
"""
Script to reproduce and test the scope handling issue for password and client_credentials grants.
"""
import os
import sys
import django

# Setup Django environment
sys.path.append('/workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7')
os.environ.setdefault('DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE', 'oidc_provider.tests.settings')
django.setup()

from django.test import TestCase, override_settings
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from oidc_provider.models import Client, Token
from oidc_provider.tests.app.utils import create_fake_user, create_fake_client
import json

class ScopeIssueTest(TestCase):
    
    def setUp(self):
        self.user = create_fake_user()
        self.client = create_fake_client(response_type='code', grant_type='password')
        # Set client scopes
        self.client.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'email']
        self.client.save()
        
    @override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True)
    def test_password_grant_without_scope_should_use_client_scopes(self):
        """Test that password grant without scope parameter uses client scopes."""
        from django.test import Client as TestClient
        
        test_client = TestClient()
        
        # Test password grant WITHOUT scope parameter
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'username': self.user.username,
            'password': '1234',
            'client_id': self.client.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client.client_secret,
        }
        
        print("Testing password grant WITHOUT scope parameter...")
        response = test_client.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Response content: {response.content}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_data = json.loads(response.content)
            access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
            if access_token:
                # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
                token_obj = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
                print(f"Token scopes assigned: {token_obj.scope}")
                print(f"Expected client scopes: {self.client.scope}")
                
                # This should FAIL currently - token should have client scopes but it probably doesn't
                if set(token_obj.scope) == set(self.client.scope):
                    print("✓ PASS: Token has correct client scopes")
                else:
                    print("✗ FAIL: Token does not have client scopes")
                    print(f"  Expected: {self.client.scope}")
                    print(f"  Actual: {token_obj.scope}")
            else:
                print("✗ FAIL: No access token in response")
        else:
            print("✗ FAIL: Non-200 response")
            
    @override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True)  
    def test_password_grant_with_valid_scope(self):
        """Test that password grant with valid scope parameter uses requested scopes."""
        from django.test import Client as TestClient
        
        test_client = TestClient()
        
        # Test password grant WITH valid scope parameter
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'username': self.user.username,
            'password': '1234',
            'client_id': self.client.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client.client_secret,
            'scope': 'openid profile'  # Subset of client scopes
        }
        
        print("\nTesting password grant WITH valid scope parameter...")
        response = test_client.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Response content: {response.content}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_data = json.loads(response.content)
            access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
            if access_token:
                # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
                token_obj = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
                print(f"Token scopes assigned: {token_obj.scope}")
                print(f"Requested scopes: ['openid', 'profile']")
                
                # This should PASS - token should have requested scopes
                expected_scopes = ['openid', 'profile']
                if set(token_obj.scope) == set(expected_scopes):
                    print("✓ PASS: Token has correct requested scopes")
                else:
                    print("✗ FAIL: Token does not have requested scopes")
                    print(f"  Expected: {expected_scopes}")
                    print(f"  Actual: {token_obj.scope}")
            else:
                print("✗ FAIL: No access token in response")
        else:
            print("✗ FAIL: Non-200 response")
            
    def test_client_credentials_grant_without_scope(self):
        """Test that client_credentials grant works correctly."""
        from django.test import Client as TestClient
        
        test_client = TestClient()
        
        # Test client_credentials grant
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'client_credentials',
            'client_id': self.client.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client.client_secret,
        }
        
        print("\nTesting client_credentials grant...")
        response = test_client.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Response content: {response.content}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_data = json.loads(response.content)
            access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
            scope_returned = response_data.get('scope')
            if access_token:
                # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
                token_obj = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
                print(f"Token scopes assigned: {token_obj.scope}")
                print(f"Client scopes: {self.client.scope}")
                print(f"Scope returned in response: {scope_returned}")
                
                # This should already work correctly
                if set(token_obj.scope) == set(self.client.scope):
                    print("✓ PASS: Client credentials token has correct client scopes")
                else:
                    print("✗ FAIL: Client credentials token does not have client scopes")
                    print(f"  Expected: {self.client.scope}")
                    print(f"  Actual: {token_obj.scope}")
            else:
                print("✗ FAIL: No access token in response")
        else:
            print("✗ FAIL: Non-200 response")

"""
Debug script to understand current scope behavior.
"""

import json
import sys
import os
import django

# Setup Django environment  
sys.path.append('/workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7')
os.environ.setdefault('DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE', 'oidc_provider.tests.settings')

# Configure Django
import django
from django.test.utils import setup_test_environment
setup_test_environment()

django.setup()

from django.test import TestCase, override_settings, Client
from oidc_provider.tests.app.utils import create_fake_user, create_fake_client
from oidc_provider.models import Token


class DebugScopeTest(TestCase):
    
    def setUp(self):
        # Create test data
        self.user = create_fake_user()
        self.client_obj = create_fake_client(response_type='code', grant_type='password')
        # Set client scopes
        self.client_obj.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'email']
        self.client_obj.save()
        
    @override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True)
    def test_debug_current_behavior(self):
        """Debug current password grant behavior without scope."""
        c = Client()
        
        # Test password grant WITHOUT scope parameter
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'username': self.user.username,
            'password': '1234',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
        }
        
        print("=" * 60)
        print("DEBUGGING CURRENT PASSWORD GRANT BEHAVIOR")
        print("=" * 60)
        print(f"Client scopes: {self.client_obj.scope}")
        print(f"POST data: {post_data}")
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Response content: {response.content}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_data = json.loads(response.content)
            access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
            if access_token:
                # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
                token_obj = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
                print(f"Token scopes assigned: {token_obj.scope}")
                print(f"Expected client scopes: {self.client_obj.scope}")
                
                # Check if they match
                if set(token_obj.scope) == set(self.client_obj.scope):
                    print("✓ CORRECT: Token has client scopes")
                else:
                    print("✗ ISSUE: Token does not have client scopes")
                    print(f"  Expected: {self.client_obj.scope}")
                    print(f"  Actual: {token_obj.scope}")
                    
                    # What did we get instead?
                    if len(token_obj.scope) == 0:
                        print("  Got EMPTY scope list!")
                        
        else:
            print("Request failed")
            try:
                error_data = json.loads(response.content)
                print(f"Error: {error_data}")
            except:
                print("Non-JSON error response")
                
    @override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True)
    def test_debug_with_scope_parameter(self):
        """Debug password grant behavior with scope parameter."""
        c = Client()
        
        # Test password grant WITH scope parameter
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'username': self.user.username,
            'password': '1234',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
            'scope': 'openid profile'
        }
        
        print("\n" + "=" * 60)
        print("DEBUGGING PASSWORD GRANT WITH SCOPE PARAMETER")  
        print("=" * 60)
        print(f"Client scopes: {self.client_obj.scope}")
        print(f"Requested scopes: ['openid', 'profile']")
        print(f"POST data: {post_data}")
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Response content: {response.content}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_data = json.loads(response.content)
            access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
            if access_token:
                # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
                token_obj = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
                print(f"Token scopes assigned: {token_obj.scope}")
                
                # Check if they match requested
                expected_scopes = ['openid', 'profile']
                if set(token_obj.scope) == set(expected_scopes):
                    print("✓ CORRECT: Token has requested scopes")
                else:
                    print("✗ ISSUE: Token does not have requested scopes")
                    print(f"  Expected: {expected_scopes}")
                    print(f"  Actual: {token_obj.scope}")
                        
        else:
            print("Request failed")
            try:
                error_data = json.loads(response.content)
                print(f"Error: {error_data}")
            except:
                print("Non-JSON error response")

    def test_debug_client_credentials(self):
        """Debug client_credentials grant behavior."""
        c = Client()
        
        # Test client_credentials grant
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'client_credentials',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
        }
        
        print("\n" + "=" * 60)
        print("DEBUGGING CLIENT_CREDENTIALS GRANT")  
        print("=" * 60)
        print(f"Client scopes: {self.client_obj.scope}")
        print(f"POST data: {post_data}")
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Response content: {response.content}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_data = json.loads(response.content)
            access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
            scope_returned = response_data.get('scope')
            if access_token:
                # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
                token_obj = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
                print(f"Token scopes assigned: {token_obj.scope}")
                print(f"Scope returned in response: {scope_returned}")
                
                # Check if they match client scopes
                if set(token_obj.scope) == set(self.client_obj.scope):
                    print("✓ CORRECT: Client credentials token has client scopes")
                else:
                    print("✗ ISSUE: Client credentials token does not have client scopes")
                    print(f"  Expected: {self.client_obj.scope}")
                    print(f"  Actual: {token_obj.scope}")
                        
        else:
            print("Request failed")
            try:
                error_data = json.loads(response.content)
                print(f"Error: {error_data}")
            except:
                print("Non-JSON error response")

"""
Simple test to check current scope behavior.
"""
import os
import sys
import django
import json

# Setup Django
sys.path.append('/workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7')
os.environ.setdefault('DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE', 'oidc_provider.tests.settings')
django.setup()

from django.test import TestCase, override_settings
from django.test import Client
from oidc_provider.tests.app.utils import create_fake_user, create_fake_client
from oidc_provider.models import Token


class CheckScopesTest(TestCase):
    
    def setUp(self):
        self.user = create_fake_user()
        self.client_obj = create_fake_client(response_type='code', grant_type='password')
        # Set client scopes
        self.client_obj.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'email']
        self.client_obj.save()
        
    @override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True)
    def test_check_password_grant_scopes_without_scope_param(self):
        """Check what scopes are assigned when no scope param is provided."""
        c = Client()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'username': self.user.username,
            'password': '1234',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
        }
        
        # Remove scope parameter to simulate the issue
        if 'scope' in post_data:
            del post_data['scope']
            
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_data = json.loads(response.content)
            access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
            if access_token:
                token_obj = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
                print(f"Client scopes: {self.client_obj.scope}")
                print(f"Token scopes: {token_obj.scope}")
                print(f"Match: {set(token_obj.scope) == set(self.client_obj.scope)}")
                
                # This is the issue - when no scope is provided, we get empty scopes
                if len(token_obj.scope) == 0:
                    print("ISSUE CONFIRMED: Empty scopes assigned when no scope parameter provided")
                else:
                    print("No issue detected")
            else:
                print("No access token in response")
        else:
            print(f"Request failed: {response.content}")

from oidc_provider.lib.endpoints.token import TokenEndpoint
original_create_access_token_response_dic = TokenEndpoint.create_access_token_response_dic

def debug_create_access_token_response_dic(self):
    print('=== DEBUG INFO ===')
    print(f'params[\"scope\"]: \"{self.params[\"scope\"]}\"')
    print(f'scope split: {self.params[\"scope\"].split(\" \") if self.params[\"scope\"] else []}')
    print(f'client scope: {self.client.scope}')
    result = original_create_access_token_response_dic(self)
    return result

TokenEndpoint.create_access_token_response_dic = debug_create_access_token_response_dic

        'username': user.username,

        response_data = json.loads(response.content)

            print(f'Token scopes: {token_obj.scope}')

Let me trace through the code manually to understand the issue:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the token.py file, I can see the issue clearly now:

In the `create_access_token_response_dic` method (lines 231-258), when handling password grants:

Line 237: `self.params['scope'].split(' ')` 

When no scope parameter is provided in the request, `self.params['scope']` will be an empty string `''`. 
So `''.split(' ')` returns `['']` (a list with one empty string), not an empty list.

This means the token gets assigned a scope containing one empty string element, rather than using the client's scopes.

For client_credentials, it correctly uses `self.client.scope` (line 266).

The fix should be:
1. When no scope is provided, use client scopes for both password and client_credentials grants
2. When scope is provided, validate it against client scopes
3. Add scope validation and error handling
4. Add optional scope display in introspection response

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I understand the issue completely. Let me summarize the problems and solution:

### 5.1 Problem Statement:
1. **Password Grant Issue**: When no `scope` parameter is provided, `self.params['scope']` is an empty string. Calling `.split(' ')` on an empty string returns `['']` (list with one empty string), not an empty list. This causes tokens to be created with incorrect scopes.
2. **Missing Scope Validation**: There's no validation to check if requested scopes are supported by the client for password grants.
3. **Inconsistent Behavior**: client_credentials correctly uses `self.client.scope`, but password grants don't.
4. **Missing Introspection Scope Feature**: There's no way to optionally include scope in introspection responses.

### 5.2 Problem Location:
- `/oidc_provider/lib/endpoints/token.py` - Lines 237, 130-133, and validation logic
- `/oidc_provider/lib/endpoints/introspection.py` - Missing scope inclusion logic
- `/oidc_provider/settings.py` - Missing new setting

### 5.3 How to Fix:
1. **Fix Password Grant Scope Handling**: Modify `create_access_token_response_dic()` to use client scopes when no scope parameter is provided
2. **Add Scope Validation**: Add validation for both password and client_credentials grants
3. **Add New Setting**: Add `OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE` setting
4. **Enhance Introspection**: Modify introspection endpoint to optionally include scope

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION<tool_call>task_tracker<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response># Task List

4. ⏳ Add scope validation for client_credentials grant_type

5. ⏳ Add new setting OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE

6. ⏳ Modify introspection endpoint to optionally include scope in response

7. ⏳ Create comprehensive test to verify all scenarios work correctly

        # See https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-4.3

        # Handle optional scope parameter - if not provided, use client scopes
        if self.params['scope']:
            requested_scopes = self.params['scope'].split(' ')
        else:
            requested_scopes = self.client.scope

        # Validate requested scopes against client scopes
        if requested_scopes:
            invalid_scopes = set(requested_scopes) - set(self.client.scope)
            if invalid_scopes:
                raise TokenError('invalid_scope')

        token = create_token(
            self.user,
            self.client,
            requested_scopes)

        id_token_dic = create_id_token(
            token=token,
            user=self.user,
            aud=self.client.client_id,
            nonce='self.code.nonce',
            at_hash=token.at_hash,
            request=self.request,
            scope=token.scope,
        )

        token.id_token = id_token_dic
        token.save()

        return {
            'access_token': token.access_token,
            'refresh_token': token.refresh_token,
            'expires_in': settings.get('OIDC_TOKEN_EXPIRE'),
            'token_type': 'bearer',

from django.test import TestCase, override_settings
from django.test import Client
from oidc_provider.tests.app.utils import create_fake_user, create_fake_client
from oidc_provider.models import Token


class ComprehensiveScopeTest(TestCase):
    
    def setUp(self):
        self.user = create_fake_user()
        self.client_obj = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
        # Set client scopes
        self.client_obj.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'email']
        self.client_obj.save()
        
    @override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True)
    def test_password_grant_without_scope_uses_client_scopes(self):
        """Test that password grant without scope parameter uses client scopes."""
        c = Client()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'username': self.user.username,
            'password': '1234',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        
        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
        response_data = json.loads(response.content)
        access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
        self.assertIsNotNone(access_token)
        
        # Check that token has client scopes
        token_obj = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
        self.assertEqual(set(token_obj.scope), set(self.client_obj.scope))
        print(f"✓ Password grant without scope: Token scopes {token_obj.scope} match client scopes {self.client_obj.scope}")
        
    @override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True)
    def test_password_grant_with_valid_scope(self):
        """Test that password grant with valid scope parameter uses requested scopes."""
        c = Client()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'username': self.user.username,
            'password': '1234',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
            'scope': 'openid profile'
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        
        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
        response_data = json.loads(response.content)
        access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
        self.assertIsNotNone(access_token)
        
        # Check that token has requested scopes
        token_obj = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
        expected_scopes = ['openid', 'profile']
        self.assertEqual(set(token_obj.scope), set(expected_scopes))
        print(f"✓ Password grant with valid scope: Token scopes {token_obj.scope} match requested scopes {expected_scopes}")
        
    @override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True)
    def test_password_grant_with_invalid_scope_raises_error(self):
        """Test that password grant with invalid scope raises TokenError."""
        c = Client()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'username': self.user.username,
            'password': '1234',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
            'scope': 'openid invalid_scope'
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        
        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 400)
        response_data = json.loads(response.content)
        self.assertEqual(response_data.get('error'), 'invalid_scope')
        print("✓ Password grant with invalid scope correctly raises invalid_scope error")
        
    def test_client_credentials_without_scope_uses_client_scopes(self):
        """Test that client_credentials grant without scope parameter uses client scopes."""
        c = Client()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'client_credentials',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        
        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
        response_data = json.loads(response.content)
        access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
        scope_returned = response_data.get('scope')
        self.assertIsNotNone(access_token)
        
        # Check that token has client scopes
        token_obj = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
        self.assertEqual(set(token_obj.scope), set(self.client_obj.scope))
        self.assertEqual(scope_returned, ' '.join(self.client_obj.scope))
        print(f"✓ Client credentials without scope: Token scopes {token_obj.scope} match client scopes {self.client_obj.scope}")
        
    def test_client_credentials_with_valid_scope(self):
        """Test that client_credentials grant with valid scope parameter uses requested scopes."""
        c = Client()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'client_credentials',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
            'scope': 'openid profile'
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        
        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
        response_data = json.loads(response.content)
        access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
        scope_returned = response_data.get('scope')
        self.assertIsNotNone(access_token)
        
        # Check that token has requested scopes
        token_obj = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
        expected_scopes = ['openid', 'profile']
        self.assertEqual(set(token_obj.scope), set(expected_scopes))
        self.assertEqual(scope_returned, 'openid profile')
        print(f"✓ Client credentials with valid scope: Token scopes {token_obj.scope} match requested scopes {expected_scopes}")
        
    def test_client_credentials_with_invalid_scope_raises_error(self):
        """Test that client_credentials grant with invalid scope raises TokenError."""
        c = Client()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'client_credentials',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
            'scope': 'openid invalid_scope'
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        
        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 400)
        response_data = json.loads(response.content)
        self.assertEqual(response_data.get('error'), 'invalid_scope')
        print("✓ Client credentials with invalid scope correctly raises invalid_scope error")

    @override_settings(OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE=True, OIDC_INTROSPECTION_VALIDATE_AUDIENCE_SCOPE=False)
    def test_introspection_includes_scope_when_enabled(self):
        """Test that introspection includes scope when OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE is True."""
        # First create a token via password grant
        c = Client()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'username': self.user.username,
            'password': '1234',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
            'scope': 'openid profile'
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
        response_data = json.loads(response.content)
        access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
        self.assertIsNotNone(access_token)
        
        # Now introspect the token
        introspect_client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
        introspect_client.scope = ['token_introspection']
        introspect_client.save()
        
        introspect_data = {
            'token': access_token,
            'client_id': introspect_client.client_id,
            'client_secret': introspect_client.client_secret,
        }
        
        introspect_response = c.post('/openid/introspect/', introspect_data)
        self.assertEqual(introspect_response.status_code, 200)
        introspect_data = json.loads(introspect_response.content)
        
        # Should include scope when enabled
        self.assertIn('scope', introspect_data)
        self.assertEqual(introspect_data['scope'], 'openid profile')
        print("✓ Introspection correctly includes scope when OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE=True")
        
    @override_settings(OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE=False, OIDC_INTROSPECTION_VALIDATE_AUDIENCE_SCOPE=False)
    def test_introspection_excludes_scope_when_disabled(self):
        """Test that introspection excludes scope when OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE is False."""
        # First create a token via password grant
        c = Client()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'username': self.user.username,
            'password': '1234',
            'client_id': self.client_obj.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client_obj.client_secret,
            'scope': 'openid profile'
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', post_data)
        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
        response_data = json.loads(response.content)
        access_token = response_data.get('access_token')
        self.assertIsNotNone(access_token)
        
        # Now introspect the token
        introspect_client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
        introspect_client.scope = ['token_introspection']
        introspect_client.save()
        
        introspect_data = {
            'token': access_token,
            'client_id': introspect_client.client_id,
            'client_secret': introspect_client.client_secret,
        }
        
        introspect_response = c.post('/openid/introspect/', introspect_data)
        self.assertEqual(introspect_response.status_code, 200)
        introspect_data = json.loads(introspect_response.content)
        
        # Should NOT include scope when disabled
        self.assertNotIn('scope', introspect_data)
        print("✓ Introspection correctly excludes scope when OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE=False")
